ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u4	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, Sir! I ask you a question and a problem that affects many local governments and CSR activists, and concerns the purchase of a used fire truck outside our country. The case boils down to the very high costs incurred by TSOs for obtaining different types of certification, approval, vehicle authorisation and use for rescue operations. These additional costs imposed on TSOs lead to the fact that such purchase of a fire-fighting vehicle abroad becomes insolvent, while the municipalities cannot afford to purchase a new fire-fighting vehicle and consequently the residents remain with less fire protection. Therefore, I have questions: Can fire cars obtained from European Union countries be admitted to rescue and fire-fighting operations without additional certification? The second question: Will the provisions requiring the authorisation of fire-fighting vehicles purchased by units of TSOs abroad or obtained in the framework of an international partnership be relaxed? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u5	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The issue of admission to fire-fighting operations purchased in the European Union is mainly related to the problem of costs. Today, the cost of such certification ranges from 30 to 80,000. zł. It's a huge amount of money. This cost consists of an initial fee, the cost of testing the car, the cost of analysis of documentation and the cost of assessing the organisational and technical conditions. It seems that these are not entirely reasonable costs. Therefore, I have a question: Is it possible to significantly reduce the costs of certification of such cars? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u6	Thank you very much. And the Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u7	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Members, This problem, which today is the subject of a question on current issues, has been repeatedly reported in recent years, especially by local authorities, but also by the voluntary fire department itself, which brings from abroad used fire cars purchased or transferred to them in other ways, e.g. within the framework of an international partnership. These high costs of obtaining admission certificates have been indicated. Because it is such a formula - not certification, just a certificate of approval - in accordance with the rules that are currently in force. I'm gonna talk about these costs right away. These costs which you have called up are excessive. According to the data we have, they are high, but not that high, ranging from 26 to 32,000. zł, which is also a large amount, of course, but here almost twice or even more than twice these costs have been overestimated. I don't know where that data came from. I would like to remind you that according to the article. Paragraph 7 1 Fire Protection Act products intended to ensure public safety or health and life protection and property put into service in fire protection units may be used and used only after prior authorisation. A detailed list of these products is contained in the Regulation of the Minister for the Interior and Administration of 20 June 2007 on the list of products intended to ensure public safety or the protection of health and life and property, as well as the rules for the authorisation of such products for use. The provisions that are in force today take into account the circumstances relating to risks that may arise if they deviate from those procedures for obtaining the certificate of admission. It must be borne in mind that the requirements for fire cars vary from one European country to another. It is also about ensuring the compatibility of these vehicles, those fire-fighting vehicles that are imported, with our system, with our products commonly used in our fire-fighting system. It also happens - and this must not be excluded or forgotten - that imported vehicles are subject to various modifications. These modifications may have a significant impact on the performance of vehicles, on the safety of crews themselves, on firefighters who will use them, and on the effectiveness of firefighters who will use these cars. Such an example, which can be cited here, is quite clear, which is probably also a good picture of what I am talking about, are cars imported from the UK with steering systems placed on a different side than in vehicles used in Poland. In conversions, a structural breach may occur when the steering wheel is shifted to the left side. This can affect the efficiency of the vehicle, etc. I don't have time to talk about it in detail, because it would have to be described in a much more detailed way, but all this is what causes them to be introduced - regulations - these limitations. They are based on the fact that a car imported from abroad must undergo these tests and obtain a certificate of admission issued by the Science and Research Centre for Fire Protection - the State Research Institute. Chief of State Fire Department in connection with these demands of local governments and voluntary firefighters has set up a team working on this case. The team at this stage rather recommends maintaining these certifications of admission, i.e. maintaining those criteria that apply today, which ensure the safety of these vehicles and their efficiency, as well as, I repeat, compatibility with the equipment used in Poland. But because there are also arguments that you Members are calling to mind, the arguments that are undoubtedly real, correct, we have once again decided to take a very thorough look at all of this. I do not want to declare clearly the results of this work at the moment, but we will once again take into account all the circumstances, also the fact that there is indeed a high demand from local governments and voluntary firefighters in terms of the possibility of taking over these cars from abroad. Perhaps these provisions will consequently be slightly relaxed, but I do not want to go into details on how to do so at the moment. Of course, it is necessary, in these initial concepts, to diversify the approach to new cars and second-hand cars. It is certainly important to aim for a reduction in costs. The ways to reduce costs are today, but they, these mechanisms, of course, only work within certain limits, so we will continue to work on it. That I can declare in the conclusion of my answer. This is where we've been consulting freshly on all of this. I can say that there's a possibility that we can verify and soften these rules. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u25	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Actually, the answer to this question is already in the question itself, because the rules here speak unambiguously, namely people with other citizenship who study in Poland and have the Polish Charter - and yet there is, I will not mention it now, a list of foreigners who meet the relevant requirements - study on the principles of Polish citizens and are then entitled to receive scholarships, among others. also social scholarships. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u41	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr President, thank you very much for this question, because it also gives us a chance to talk a little bit about the activities of the Ministry of Digitalization in this area. First of all, I would like to stress that the mDocuments project responds to the real need that arises in our society and not just ours, but in general we are talking about people who appreciate, above all, the mobile way of life and the facilitation of everyday activities. And the thing is, their expectation is that the only thing they carry around is a phone, not necessarily a wallet. So we're talking about things that affect people's everyday lives. And the mDocuments project introduces a service that is an optional service, we are not talking about any introduction of an obligation, but as if giving such an option to a citizen, so that there is a credible, universal way for an official to be able to confirm his identity, whether to identify things or legal status, or the right of a citizen without having to use documents in paper or plastic form. The service is that a device which at the moment is used by an official or an official is displayed with data from a public register which are identical to those data which are normally displayed in a traditional document. Thus, the citizen is not obliged to carry traditional documents, because access to these data which are contained in mDocuments, official or official is immediately obtained on his device. This mechanism is universal, i.e. it may apply to all documents whose data are stored in state registers, and it will be similar, which will make the introduction of further documents into the mDocuments project not require any additional changes in the area of infrastructure, whether on the part of the citizen or on the part of the authorities. The only action required at a time when a citizen wishes to use mDocuments is to provide a police officer or a telephone number or a PESEL number. On this basis, also in certain cases with additional authorisation via SMS mechanism, the official displays and is able to compare whether or not to use the data he received from the register, as well as to verify the citizen, simply comparing, as with a traditional document, the image of the face that appeared to him on a computer monitor or on his device, with the face of the citizen who stands before him, dealing with the matter. What should also be emphasised is that our solution does not replace traditional evidence or other documents. This is, as I said, an optional additional possibility to confirm, where the citizen will agree to activate and start using this service. In no way does this project interfere with the current powers of the services in the area of the legitimacy of persons and the confirmation of their identity. In this area, we do not change the legal situation in any way. The whole mDocuments project has been divided into stages to gradually supplement this offer with further documents in subsequent stages. As you said, in fact, on this one the nearest so-called The road map related to the project in addition to the initially planned identity card are the driver's license, registration and civil liability insurance as those documents that we see are most popular, and it would be the greatest facilitation resulting from the mDocuments project, most noticeable to the customer and most desirable. As for the schedule of our actions, it was indeed in May this year. The piloting of this first document, m-personal evidence, is planned, while the production implementation of this service is planned at the end of June. This term is simply dependent on the legislative work related to the legal conditions for the introduction of this service. As far as further documents are concerned, the vehicle registration and OC are the documents that we plan to implement productively by the end of 2017, in the fourth quarter, while the driving licence is planned to be implemented in the first half of next year. These are the plans already identified, while we will keep track of or verify the needs of other documents that citizens need in order to use it in this project. We assume that we will gradually be able to supplement the offer of mDocuments with further documents in cycles, I do not know, quarterly or semi-annual. So much for the answer on my part.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u82	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, She referred here to the amendment, referring to the law on the amendment of the Social Assistance Act, which was based on the parliamentary draft law on the amendment of the Social Assistance Act of 22 April 2015. It was this amendment of the law that introduced a number of changes concerning accommodation facilities for homeless people. You can say that so far, this area has not been regulated before, there have been no standards. According to the new wording of the Act, temporary shelter may be granted to homeless persons by three categories of facilities - shelter, accommodation and heating facilities have been introduced. According to this law, the amendment of this law gives delegations the introduction of standards concerning services, concerning persons, the standard of persons, the qualification of persons who provide these services, and the standard of facilities. As you have rightly pointed out, in particular the rules on the standard of facilities and the requirements contained therein result to a large extent from the Minister of Infrastructure's regulation. We could not give smaller minimum solutions at this stage. But at the same time, I would like to say that this regulation, the draft of this regulation, was based on a project that was implemented between 2009 and 2014 by most large NGOs dealing with homeless people. It was the POWER-OWS project 1.18: Creating and developing standards of assistance and social inclusion services, and this was a measure of standardising work with homeless people; developing a municipal standard of getting out of homelessness. The experience or developed standards contained in this project were used for the design of the Regulation. At the same time, I would like to stress that in accordance with Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Act on Social Assistance requires the provision of shelter, food and the necessary clothing for those deprived of it is one of the tasks of the municipalities, and municipal authorities are obliged to carry out the tasks in this regard. Solutions concerning shelter facilities for homeless people, i.e. in this proposed regulation we are talking about, concern those institutions that enter the social assistance system, i.e. institutions whose activities are financed by municipalities, i.e. run by local government units or run on behalf of local government units - institutions with which local government units may have contracts concluded. At the same time, if we look at the history of the creation of these standards within this project, we will notice that the organisations indicated that if these three categories of facilities were introduced: shelter, accommodation, heating room, they would not meet, for example. the standard of accommodation, they will go back one step at a time and meet the standards, heating requirements. I would like to say that at this stage we are actually at the moment before the regulation is signed. It has actually gone through the whole legislative path, we have already received it approved by the Government Legislative Centre and these standards are due to enter from 2018. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u93	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of the Environment, Mr Andrzej Szweda-Lewandowski's main conservationist of nature.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u94	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The genesis of the Natura 2000 network was observed at the end of the 1970s as a phenomenon of disappearance or reduction in the number of many species of birds. The purpose of tackling this phenomenon was precisely the implementation in 1979 of the Birds Directive, covering the protection of all species of birds and introducing part of the protection areas to ensure that this objective is achieved. As it has become apparent over the years that, despite the introduction of this directive, there are still clearly negative developments, it has been decided to widen the network of protected areas by the implementation of the so-called "protective areas." the Habitat Directive, the Habitats Directive and the designation of protected habitats, as well as habitats of protected species, to ensure that the basic objectives, such as the maintenance or adaptation of the population of avian species in the European Union are achieved at a level that ensures their survival or persistence and the maintenance or restoration of habitats of species other than birds but relevant to the Community, taking into account economic, social and cultural requirements. Using adequate funding, as well as human and institutional resources and knowledge, the following actions have been adopted under these two Directives: the designation of the Natura 2000 network in accordance with those Directives, the establishment of the necessary conservation measures for each Natura 2000 area, the restoration of habitats for species in and outside Natura 2000 areas, another objective: to monitor the presence of quantitatively protected species and the quality of their habitats. Based on these activities, based on specific monitoring data, it was intended to determine whether the Natura 2000 network is coherent, whether habitats are well protected and reproduced, whether species are protected and used by humans in a good and sustainable way. As you can see, Natura 2000 was primarily intended to be an instrument used to monitor the environment, the condition of habitats and maintain the well-being of these habitats through their use and not passive protection. The European Commission has been working for a long time to review the functioning of the Birds Directive and the Habitats Directive. At the moment, the opinion is being given, the consulted document Mon. A review of the adequacy of EU legislation on nature. This document shows with absolute clarity that the objectives pursued have not been achieved, so we do not know whether the Natura 2000 network is coherent or whether specific designated habitats are well protected, where they are possibly reproduced. We do not know whether species are protected well and in a balanced way, whether they are used in a sustainable way. The document also does not mention whether we have reliable data on particular species and protected habitats, or whether the public's awareness of protected areas and species is increasing or not. Based on the experience of Poland from the last year we can say that it is quite the opposite. The awareness of the European Union's society is increasingly distorted and completely inconsistent with the objectives of Nature 2000 and therefore the objectives of both the Birds and the Habitats Directives. New and disinformation completely destroys the meaning of Nature 2000. Protection comes down to two concepts: don't cut out, don't kill, and man is the greatest enemy of natural resources. Poland has seen this in the case of habitats and species important for the Community in the economic forests of the Białowieża Forest. The international pressure has resulted in the cessation of protective measures in the form of clippings in the form of tree diseases, resulting in avalanche extinction of former stands and, consequently, the disappearance of habitats and species important for the Community. What is the current state of the Natura 2000 network in Poland in terms of numbers, area? The Natura 2000 network in Poland currently occupies a total of about 20% of the country's area, consisting of 145 areas of special protection for birds and 849 areas of Community importance, in other words habitat areas. In total, there are 987 Natura 2000 sites in the country, of which approximately 56% are forested. This is proof that the forest management carried out over the last decades has been a sustainable economy, an economy moving towards sustainable management of forest resources, so as not to lose species valuable to the Community and their habitats. Agriculturally used areas occupy about 23% surface of Nature 2000, meadows and moorings - approx. 14% , wet and anthropogenic areas - about 1%. The Natura 2000 network area in marine areas is approximately 7236 km2. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u226	Thank you, Marshal. High Izbo! Dear Marshal, I have the honour to present a report from the Committee on Culture and Media on the work on the parliamentary draft resolution on enhancing the concern for sobriety of the nation. During the work of the committee, Małgorzata Zwierzan presented the problem of alcoholism, which affects more and more people every year and helps to consolidate negative social attitudes. The Committee on Culture and Media after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 23 February this year. She did not object to the resolution and adopted the resolution. In Poland, alcohol abuse is a social scourge. Statistics say as much as 14%. adult Poles drink risky and harmful, and nearly a million are addicts. It is not surprising to see such a tragic situation when many treat alcohol as part of their daily lives. As a society, we consume more and more. Over the past 6 years, alcohol consumption per person has increased from 7.6 to 9.4 l per 100 percent alcohol, a threat to national existence. In public research, more than 70% The respondents pointed out that he most often drinks alcohol without any opportunity, and that alcohol is a dangerous, treacherous substance that addicts and can lead to diseases, to loss of life, and ultimately to death. Studies show that in the last 30 days, at least every fifth grade student in the third grade and every third grade in the second year of high school got drunk at least once. As a result, 11,000 people die from alcohol. Poles a year. According to available data, Poles drink more and more, especially young people. The value of indirect social costs related to alcohol abuse amounts to about PLN 40 billion. That's twice as much as the receipts to the excise and VAT budget. For the treatment of addiction itself, its complications, it seems to be about PLN 0.5 billion per year, which makes every Polish taxpayer spend about PLN 800 per person annually to cover losses resulting from excessive alcohol consumption. However, from various sources there is information about the widespread disregard by local authorities of the laws on sobriety education and the prevention of alcoholism. There are no effective mechanisms for punishing, which explains the impunity of those who, instead of caring for public health, care for the interests of a narrow group earning money on alcohol. According to public health experts' estimates, at least 2 million children live in families with a drama of addiction. Chronic tension and stress, in which daily they are forced to function, various traumatic violence experiences, including sexual ones, cause profound destructive changes in their psyche, guilt, fear and shame. The world in which they live does not satisfy their basic needs, does not teach them what love, respect, or tolerance is. Alcohol abuse is one of the key factors involved in this situation. Those children who are raised by sober parents will be sober and will have a better chance of real and more lasting happiness. Their self-esteem and self-awareness will be so high and well established that they will not duplicate negative patterns. In view of the enormous scale of the alcoholic problem in our homeland, the Committee on Culture and Media, convinced of the particular importance and role of sobriety in social life, it sets out a resolution to the High House on enhancing the concern for sobriety of the nation, as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on increasing the concern for sobriety of the Nation. The consumption of alcohol by Poles is constantly increasing. Research confirms that alcohol abuse causes huge economic losses in the state budget, many times exceeding ad hoc profits. The victims of alcohol abuse and alcoholic disease are not only sick people, but whole families in whom the youngest suffer the most. The rule is that a family member's alcoholism becomes the epicenter of his entire family life. Its members, focusing on helping an addict to counteract the negative effects of addiction, neglect other responsibilities, especially parental responsibility. Moreover, consumption among young women, including future mothers, is steadily increasing. Sobriety is a way of thinking that allows you to maintain and develop your freedom. Being sober means being free. Sobriety, freedom from alcohol is a good thing that serves the self-realization of every human being. In Poland's history, concern for sobriety was associated with the pursuit of freedom, the rebirth of the nation, as well as defending the dignity of Poles. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland calls for increased care about sobriety of the Nation and calls on the Government of the Republic of Poland, local governments, churches, religious unions and NGOs to take systematic action in 2017 to care for sobriety of the Nation. A man can do anything today, but not everything serves his life. So much for a resolution. Thank you very much and I ask the High Sejm to adopt this resolution. [[Applause]]

[[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 13 minutes 49 hours 14 min 17]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 15 of the agenda: First reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act (Print No. 1296). I would like to ask Mr Tomasz Romekowski to provide a justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u260	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code submitted by the Kukiz Club15 is a project designed to amend the article. 505 Civil Procedure Code by adding two paragraphs to it. The change seems to appear to be minor, but for proceedings in the electronic reproving procedure, this change will be significant. The electronic reminder procedure was introduced under the provisions of the amendment of 9 January 2009, with effect from 1 January 2010. In order to be in practice, this required the creation of an electronic system and a technical infrastructure which were developed under the implementing regulations issued only in December 2009. Electronic proceedings have been standardized in the Code of Civil Procedure as a separate procedure of an optional nature. According to the intention of the legislator set out in the explanatory memorandum of the bill, a new type of judicial proceedings, in which the possibility of using modern ICT technology was created, was intended to contribute, among others. to improve judicial proceedings, reduce its costs and relieve the courts who recognise cases in traditional judicial proceedings. One court - Lublin-West District Court in Lublin, 6th Civil Division is competent for the recognition of all cases in the EPU at first instance. As in traditional reprimanding proceedings, only monetary claims and, as a rule, undisputed nature can be investigated also in the EPU. There should be no doubt that the far-reaching computerisation of procedural activities in electronic reprehensible proceedings is really translated into improving the efficiency of judicial proceedings and at the same time greatly simplifies and accelerates lawsuits. This is demonstrated by the considerable efficiency of e-court proceedings, in which almost 1.2 million cases were identified during the first 1.5 years of the law, about 1.4 million of filed actions. About 11.8 million applications were filed in e-court in 2010 and of which in 2015 - approx. 2.4 million. At that time about 10.3 million payment orders were issued, of which in 2015 - about 2 million. The computerisation of legal proceedings, its simplification and reduction of costs is also a tool for extending the guaranteed Article. 45 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland the right of access to the court. The EPU has opened a judicial path to thousands of reasons why ordinary redress would be inefficient, not only for financial reasons but also for time. It is estimated that around 30% of the more than 1 million cases that entered the e-court during the first 8 months of 2011, would never have reached the finals in court if the EPU had not been introduced. In an electronic reminder procedure, the plaintiff obtains a payment order after giving him the Feasibility Clause, on the basis of which the creditor can claim his rights by means of enforcement proceedings, after fulfilling a number of conditions required for this redress procedure. In accordance with the applicable law, opposition to an order for payment issued in an electronic reminder procedure results in the loss of all the power of the decision against all defendants, even if the opposition was brought only by one of several defendants or if the defendant raised an objection only on part of the order for payment. The Act does not provide for the possibility of objecting to part of the order. The exclusion of such a possibility seems doubtful in terms of electronic reproving, since it functions in traditional reproving proceedings. The order for payment issued as a result of the ordinary form of electronic proceedings is hereby repealed only in the part of the opposition under appeal. In view of the wording of the current regulation, the defendant is forced to question the whole decision even if, to some extent, he agrees with the merits of the action, and this solution is contrary to the principle of procedural economics. Current wording of the article. 505 of the Civil Procedure Code may also lead to a situation in which the claimant whose claim would be challenged only to a minor extent by the defendant will extend the way to obtain a final decision, which infringes the order for a civil case to be recognised by the court without undue delay. To object to a payment order which was issued in an electronic reminder procedure only by one of the defendants or to challenge only part of the decision shall always result in the execution of the order in its entirety and in respect of all defendants, i.e. Even those who didn't object. Content of the provision of the Article. 505 of the Civil Procedure Code is contrary to Article. 45 Constitution of the Republic of Poland and Art. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 The Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, which gives the opportunity to act alone before a court, which is one of the grounds and guarantees for a fair process of social justice in a democratic legal state. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 505 of the Civil Procedure Code also conflicts with the right to challenge decisions given at first instance - Article. 78 constitutions - which include: the right of discretion to challenge and review those decisions. This is because the legislator imposes the following solution on the defendant: acceptance of the content of the order in its entirety by all defendants and thus acceptance of the claimant's claim in its entirety or the need to challenge it in its entirety, even if part of the plaintiff's claim is uncontested and the defendants do not wish to pursue the process in this respect as a result of opposition to the order for payment. The concept of the whole order should refer in a clear way to the whole claim and to the cumulation in question, but in the existing version it is possible to take account of the cumulation of both the entity and the person concerned. The amendment introduced will ensure that the provision is compatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and that it is possible to carry out a clear and consistent procedure contained in other parts of the act of interpretation. From now on, objecting to an order for payment in an electronic reminder procedure by one of the defendants will not automatically result in the execution of the order against all defendants. At the same time, the amendment will provide better protection for the claimant's claims, which, in the event of an effective opposition by only one of the defendants, will be able to obtain a declaration of enforceability and enforce his claim. I would now like to refer to the comments that have been made to the print project 1296. The Supreme Court welcomed the proposed amendment, and the proposal described as a necessary correction of the clumsy amendment of the Article. 505 Code of civil procedure. The Supreme Court also notes that the proposed text of the amendment is equivalent to the Article. 505 § 2 The Code of Civil Procedure and it is part of the principle of formal availability, as it allows for greater flexibility in the use of means of challenging judgments, and the provision in the current wording limits the independence of the parties to proceedings. We are extremely surprised at the position, the opinion of the Supreme Bar Council, which clearly evaluates the project in a negative way. The Supreme Bar Council indicates that Article. 505 The Civil Procedure Code in its current version is a reasonable compromise between the request for speed of judicial proceedings and the need to protect the defendant in connection with the applicant's unreliability. The Supreme Bar Council refers to media reports, which show that the plaintiffs intentionally give incorrect addresses to the defendants and the defendants learn that payment orders have been issued against them only at the stage of enforcement proceedings. However, at this point I would like to remind you that electronic reproving is not necessary for such actions, because such actions may also occur in ordinary reproving or in prescriptive proceedings. I would also like to remind you that the Code of Civil Procedure for each of the above mentioned. The proceedings shall specify the mechanisms which are designed to safeguard the interests of the defendant. Here I point to the content of the article. 502 § 1 of the Civil Procedure Code, Art. 492 Code of Civil Procedure or Art. 168 c.p.c. Please also note that the provision of the article. 505 of the Civil Procedure Code in the current version does not contain a guarantee leading to the protection of defendants, for which the plaintiff indicated incorrect address data. This guarantee shall be contained in Article. 505 § 1 of the Code of Civil Procedure, referring to the ordinary reprimanding procedure and specifying the exact same mechanism. A payment order may not be issued if the defendant's whereabouts are unknown or if the payment order cannot be served in the country. In that case, the court shall ex officio waive the order for payment issued. Undoubtedly, the repeal of the order destroys all its legal effects. This arrangement, designed to protect the debtor, aligns the defendant's procedural situation in the proceedings in any case where there is an obstacle to the delivery of the order for payment in the country. The court shall, on receipt of confirmation that the order for payment cannot be served, waive it. Complementing formal deficiencies, e.g. by giving the defendant's current address, at the stage of the court's decision, it may not take place. In addition, please note that no one, including the Supreme Council of Attorneys, is questioning the solutions adopted on the basis of ordinary reprimanding proceedings. I would remind you that, until 1990, in the event of an objection against a payment order issued in the ordinary reprimand procedure, this order fell in its entirety. It was only the amendment of the Civil Procedure Code made in 1990 that the order for payment expired in the contested part of the opposition. The opposition of only one of the joint defendants for the same claim and of one or some of the claims taken into account results in the execution of the order only as regards them. This provision has been working successfully for 27 years and has not been revised since. Hypothesis concerning Article. 505 § The second sentence of the Civil Procedure Code covers two cases, and its content is, in fact, the following: the opposition of one of the joint defendants only to the same claim and the opposition to one or some of the claims taken into account results in the execution of the order only as regards them. In both cases, the opposition shall have identical effects, namely the loss of power of the order for payment only as regards them, i.e. the defendant who lodged the opposition, and his claim to which the opposition relates. Objection against the order for payment in the reprehensible proceedings is a restrictive appeal, which means that the case is returned to the initial stage as if the contested decision had not been given. The proceedings that have taken place since its submission are no longer a warning procedure. I point out that it is also wrong to charge the Supreme Bar Council as being added § 3 was intended to lead to inconsistencies in the legal system and to differences in the situation of those objecting to the warning procedure and those objecting to it in electronic reprimanding proceedings. As already indicated above, the hypothesis concerning Article. 505 § The second sentence of the Civil Procedure Code covers two cases, and its content is, in fact, the following: the opposition of one of the joint defendants only to the same claim and the opposition to one or some of the claims taken into account results in the execution of the order only as regards them. In both cases, the opposition has the same effect. The proposed regulation will thus lead to the harmonisation and alignment of the procedural situation of the defendants in the ordinary prosecution procedure with those who appear before the e-court as defendant. In addition, I remind you of the government bill amending certain laws in order to facilitate the recovery of claims which, according to its authors, is intended to strengthen the rights and guarantees for creditors. I point out that the Kukiz15 project aims to strengthen the rights and guarantees for creditors. Ladies and gentlemen, doctrine and case law have long been calling for this amendment, which will certainly contribute to strengthening the efficiency of electronic reproving and equalizing the situation of the defendants who, after all, do not choose whether the plaintiff will bring an action against them in ordinary reproving proceedings or in electronic proceedings. For this reason, I ask for the support of the above-party parties for the further process of the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u262	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! The project mainly addresses two issues in the field of electronic reprimanding. This concerns the possibility of repealing the order for payment only in part in a situation where the order itself will be challenged in part or challenged by only one of the co-debtors. This is a solution that is modelled on solutions from other special proceedings in civil proceedings, for example in ordinary reprimand proceedings. However, it must be borne in mind who is most often a party to electronic reprimanding. Always on the cause side is a large entity, some entrepreneur, often a debt collection company, which on the basis of the assignment bought the receivables wholesale, and on the other side practically always the consumer. This is due, for example, to the fact that the lodging of actions in electronic reproving proceedings involves electronic access and authorisation. In this situation, it is often the case that only one person appeals against the judgment or objects in the case of a reprimand, and this would result in a situation where the order for payment in the part concerning the person who has not complained of the order would be valid. The draft amends the electronic reminder procedure to the extent that it concerns the consequences of objecting to the order for payment. Added § 2 and § 3 will cause that if one of the defendants objects correctly, the order for payment will cease to be valid only in relation to that of the defendants who effectively lodged the opposition. The current regulation is contrary to Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland to the extent that the necessity to object to the entire order for payment may lead to a delay in the settlement of the case without undue delay. The right to challenge decisions given at first instance assumes inter alia. the right of discretion to challenge and review those decisions. The current regulation imposes the following solution on the defendant: either acceptance of the content of the order in full by all defendants and acceptance of the claimant's claim in full, or the need to challenge the order in full, even if part of the plaintiff's claim is uncontested between the parties and the defendants do not wish to proceed in this respect as a result of opposition to the order for payment. Such a solution is contrary to the Constitution and should be adapted as soon as possible to the standards that should apply in a democratic state of law. In other words, if he brought an action in full, he could say that he did not question the claim for a certain amount or even accept the claim for a certain amount. This means that there is a contradiction in the proposed wording of these provisions. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 505 c.p.c. relating to the ordinary proceedings of the reprehensible, in its § 1 is referred to as a loss of power to object without indicating the extent of the loss of that power. Unprecise is also § 2 in a project the content of which may lead to contradiction and consequently to a lack of proper understanding with regard to Article. 73 § 2 c.p.c. concerning joint participation in the single market. Comments also concern § 3 because it is possible to object to the claim - the claim is contested and, therefore, we question all or part of the claim by objecting. Thus, in § 3, there should be a provision: as to the part of the claim and not the part of the decision. Thus, in this way, the project cannot stand. I therefore hope that during the work of the committee, the wording of the provision will be verified so that it will not only correspond to the letter of law, [[♪ The bell ♪]] but he also referred to the rules provided for in the ordinary reproving procedure. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u277	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u278	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present our position on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party on the draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure. On 1 January 2010 new civil proceedings - electronic reproving procedure entered into force in the Polish legal system. This is an optional procedure because the case is pending only if the plaintiff decides to file an action by electronic means. This amendment was intended to relieve the courts of minor monetary claims brought in mass against different persons, but in identical factual situations. The 16th Civil Division was established in the District Court of Lublin, which examines actions brought electronically regardless of the value of the subject matter of the dispute, and its jurisdiction covers the whole of Poland. The case file shall be kept in electronic form and shall be made available to the parties. There's no evidence attached to the lawsuit. Online, the plaintiff pays the court fee. The plaintiff is suing for payment by filling in an electronic form. Today's project is updating the electronic reminder procedure to the extent that it concerns the consequences of the opposition to the order for payment. According to the draft, in the event of a proper objection, the order for payment shall cease to be in force in its entirety and the court shall refer the matter to the court by general jurisdiction. In that case, no supplementary fee shall be levied on the application. 505 § 1 of the project corresponding in substance to the provision in the applicable wording). The authors of the project add provisions § 2 and 3 providing that, in the event of a properly lodged objection by one of the defendants, the order for payment shall cease to be valid only in relation to that of the defendants who has effectively lodged an objection (§ 2). In the event of a proper objection, only part of the settlement of the order for payment shall cease to be valid only in respect of the part in respect of which the opposition has been effectively lodged. According to the Constitutional Court's order of 15 January 2015, Article 2 of the Constitutional Tribunal's Rules of Procedure. 505 §1 c.p.c. in the current wording is incompatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The Constitutional Tribunal states that the non-constitutionality of Article 505 § 1 c.p.c. results from the fact that, pursuant to that provision, an objection against a payment order which was issued in an electronic reminder procedure only by one of the defendants or only part of the decision will always result in the execution of the order in its entirety and in respect of all the defendants, i.e. Even those who didn't object. Content of the provision referred to in Article. 505 c.p.c. According to the Constitutional Court, it is contrary to the Article. 45 Constitution of the Republic of Poland and Art. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 The Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, which gives the opportunity to act alone before a court, which is one of the grounds and guarantees for a fair process of social justice in a democratic legal state. At present, the legislator imposes on the defendant the following: acceptance of the content of the order in its entirety by all defendants and thus acceptance of the claimant's claim in its entirety or the need to challenge it in its entirety, even if part of the plaintiff's claim is uncontested and the defendants do not wish to proceed in this respect as a result of opposition to the order for payment. The current editing of the rule may lead to unfair settlements, including those of the law. to shake the order for payment by one of the co-defendants with effect for all of them, even against their will. It thus limits the independence of the parties to the proceedings and their right to court. High Izbo, this project is an attempt to correct the errors of the previous amendment, which deals with electronic reprimanding procedures. Undoubtedly, the rules currently in force in this area need to be improved because of their unconstitutionality. The proposed amendments will allow for greater flexibility in the use of remedies. They will adapt the provisions of electronic reprimanding procedures to traditional reprimanding procedures and, above all, restore participants to their rights, independent of co-defendants' actions. At the same time, the amendment will provide better protection for the claimant's claims, which, in the event of an effective opposition by only one of the defendants, will be able to obtain a declaration of enforceability and enforce his claim. Ladies and gentlemen, the PSL Members' Club is in favour of directing the project to further work.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u280	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the main principles of the bill on the Poznań Metropolitan Union. The project assumes the possibility of creating a metropolitan union in the area of Wielkopolska Voivodship, which would include Poznań as a city on the rights of the county, as well as municipalities and districts which have strong functional links with the capital of Wielkopolska. According to the Act, the union is formed by a regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers. According to Art. 6 the decision to join the union will be taken by individual municipalities' councils. The Municipal Councils will then address their proposal to the Minister responsible for public administration through the Wielkopolska Voivodeship. What is very important is one of the elements of the proposal, as provided for in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 4 will be the result of a public consultation based on the referendum formula. Art. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the proposed law indicates public tasks which may be carried out by a metropolitan union and which already go beyond the administrative boundaries of individual municipalities. These are tasks in the field of public collective transport in the area of the union, the shaping of spatial governance, the development of the area of association, cooperation in determining the course of national and voivodship roads in the area of the union, the promotion of the union and its area, as well as the role of a service centre common to local governments forming part of the metropolitan union and their units. In addition, lips. 3 of this article indicates that, on the basis of an agreement concluded with a government authority, a metropolitan union may carry out public tasks falling within the scope and operation of the government administration. Let me stress: maybe. Distribute. The 4th draft law describes the management of the metropolitan union. And so, according to art. The 18 bodies of the union are the assembly of the metropolitan union and the management of the metropolitan union. The assembly is a body constituting and controlling it, composed of delegates from the municipalities forming part of the union - one from each municipality and one from the county. Delegates are the mayors, mayors, city presidents, and the seniors or persons authorised. The Act introduces a double majority decision-making solution. The condition of obtaining a double majority is fulfilled if the majority of the statutory assembly is voted in favour of the resolution and the number of delegates representing the municipalities that the inhabitants of these municipalities are the majority of the population living in the area of the union, while the delegate of the city of Poznań weighs 40% Voices. The purpose of this provision is to safeguard the interests of all members of the union. As far as the union board is concerned, our intention is that the union board should not be political, hence the composition will be very... substantive. Art. 28 assumes that board membership cannot be combined with executive functions such as the mayor, the governor or the president of the city or the senator's mandate. We want the management not to focus on politics, but on specific tasks placed before the Poznań metropolis. In turn, the relationship according to our concept is to be an instrument for effective performance of tasks, not a political instrument. I would like to point out that the decision to set up the Poznań Metropolitan Union will allow the financial instrument referred to in the article to be used. 51b point 1 of the Act of 13 November 2003 on the income of local government units, which may amount to PLN 140 million per year in the case of the Poznań metropolitan area. This is undoubtedly a very specific incentive, which causes Poznań, Poznań County, Poznań County, Poznań County and other local government units after the creation of this legal opportunity will certainly decide to join the union. Another advantage of the law is the fact that its adoption does not result in the creation of the Poznań Metropolitan Union, but only in this possibility. The projecters pointed out that the autonomy of the municipalities surrounding Poznań, which has the status of a city on the rights of the county, was maintained. The project assumes that only municipalities that wish to join the union will become part of it. Ladies and gentlemen! By joining the draft law, we followed the logic of thinking, according to which, since there is no political will to implement the metropolitan law of a nationwide nature, we should draw up laws dedicated to specific areas - in this case it is about Poznań and its surrounding municipalities. When we discuss metropolises, we meet with different concepts relating to the metropolis. In general, however, the metropolis is, according to the adopted definition, a multifunctional centre of a given region, in which the functions of higher order are concentrated, i.e. transport, scientific or cultural functions. Thus, the metropolitan area is a coherent functionally large-city arrangement of many settlement units and areas with a high degree of urbanisation. Under Polish legislation, the concept of metropolis appeared in the Law of 27 March 2003 on spatial planning and development and in the Resolution of the Council of Ministers No 239 of 13 December 2011 on the adoption of the 2030 spatial development concept. According to the assumptions set out in this document, metropolitan criteria would meet about 8 and 10 urban centres in Poland. However, the Genesis of this document was different from the present proposal to create metropolitan unions. On the other hand, the European Research Programme for Spatial Development of Europe ESPON only identifies Warsaw and possibly Silesia as potential European metropolises according to its criteria. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u313	Thank you very much, Member. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u314	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present our position on the Parliamentary draft law on the Poznań Metropolitan Union on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party. This project assumes that the Poznan Metropolitan Union could be established in the area of Wielkopolska Voivodship. It would constitute an association of municipalities and powiats located in this voivodship (with mandatory participation of the city on the rights of the Poznań district), covering a spatially coherent area of impact of Poznań, characterised by strong functional links, inhabited by at least 600,000 inhabitants. As in the case of other associations, the association would be provided with legal personality, its system would be governed by the statutes (concluded with the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister responsible for public administration) and its independence would be subject to judicial protection. The Union would perform public tasks on its own behalf and on its own responsibility. His organs would be the assembly and the board. The establishment of this form of association, as well as the establishment and modification of its borders by identifying the participating municipalities, would take place by means of regulations of the Council of Ministers. Regulations would be issued at the request of the Minister responsible for administration or council of the municipality whose area would be within the limits of the union (through the Wielkopolska Voivodeship). In the case of a regulation on the revision of borders, it would be mandatory to obtain the opinion of the assembly of the metropolitan union. Existing inter-gminal relationships, with the consent of the Poznań Metropolitan Union (expressed by the resolution of the union assembly), could decide, by means of a resolution of their own assembly, to transfer their rights and obligations to the metropolitan union (if the subject matter of the inter-gminal relationship would be related to the performance of the tasks of the Poznan Metropolitan Union). The project also indicated that the association would carry out public tasks for municipalities - members of the inter-governmental union - transferring their rights and obligations, not forming part of the metropolitan union within a period of 3 months, starting with the acquisition of rights and obligations. After this period, it is possible to carry out tasks for these municipalities on the basis of an agreement. The metropolitan union could carry out public tasks in the area of: public collective transport in the area of the union, shaping spatial governance, development of the union area, cooperation in determining the course of national and voivodship roads in the area of the union, promoting the union and its area and acting as a centre of services common to local governments and their units. On the basis of agreements concluded with the local government unit, the association could carry out public tasks falling within the scope of the operation of the municipality, district or local government or coordinate the implementation of these tasks. They should apply the provisions of the Municipal Government Act accordingly. Furthermore, on the basis of agreements concluded with the government administration, the relationship could carry out the tasks contracted. The President of the Council of Ministers and the voivode, and in financial matters, the Regional Audit Chamber, would supervise the activities of the metropolitan union. The provisions governing its implementation are limited to a minimum by reference to the proper application of the provisions of the Regional Government Act. High Izbo! The creation of the Poznań Metropolitan Union with a budget of PLN 140 million can significantly improve the functioning of the entire Poznań agglomeration. Importantly, this 140 million PLN would not in any way reduce the budgets of local governments, but would come from the 5% income tax paid by residents of municipalities in the association. Collective transport, development of spatial governance or the determination of roads of extra-local importance are all tasks that go beyond the horizon of one municipality. A new instrument is therefore needed to improve the joint performance of these tasks. Unlike the government draft law on the Upper Silesian agglomeration, the Poznań project does not impose anything on the municipalities. It doesn't even involve creating a new union under the law. Everything is based on a voluntary principle, i.e. respect for the subjectivity of local communities. The fact that the authors did not establish a rigid geographical framework and did not restrict access to the metropolitan union to municipalities located further away from Poznań should also be taken as a plus. However, we cannot overlook the critical voices that have recently appeared in Poznań. First of all, local government officials ask why you did not consult with them on this project. The lack of local authorities' opinions is not a good indication of the preparation of such a project for them. The comments submitted by the Poznań magistrate are also correct. The project involves artificially limiting Poznań's voice in the forum of the metropolis union to 40%, while the city actually accounts for more than half of the inhabitants of the metropolitan area. Let us also add that the project entails the need to prepare a number of amendments to the laws and other related documents and to draw up implementing acts. The absence of the latter at this stage hinders the comprehensive assessment of the draft law in terms of rights, obligations, benefits, risks that may affect both the individual members of the union and the entire union. The PSL Members' Club is in favour of referring the law to further work.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u315	A motion to reject the draft law at first reading was put forward in the discussion. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u316	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a report from the Extraordinary Commission on deregulation concerning the draft parliamentary law amending the Pharmaceutical Law Act, print No 1126. The Extraordinary Commission for Deregulation recommends rejecting the parliamentary draft amendment to the Pharmaceutical Law Act, while arguing, seeing the need to include project assumptions in the ongoing work on a major revision of pharmaceutical law in the Ministry of Health. In the opinion of the committee, the project raises a wide range of controversy, mainly in the area of the impact of regulation on the wholesale and retail markets of medicines. The project in its shape can be anti-competitive and contribute to a significant increase in drug prices. The proposed rules aim at fragmentation of the market, which reduces freedom of competition. The Parliamentary draft law does not take into account the proposed regulations of the main and most important market participants, namely wholesalers, which play an important role in the pricing policy and the pharmacies system through various, not necessarily socially beneficial, commercial mechanisms. In addition, the project does not regulate the problem of the reverse drug distribution chain, i.e. export of medicines from Poland. Such a deep regulation concerning the part of the economy and the area of public life, such as health care, should be drawn up in accordance with the public and inter-ministerial consultation procedure and the proper and detailed development of the impact of the regulation, which is not possible by the parliamentary proposal. The draft law does not provide an adequate analysis of the impact of the proposed regulation on the economic interests of consumers, in particular on the prices of medicines. In the explanatory memorandum of the project, these issues should first and foremost be examined, subject to consideration. The proposed regulations allow the fear that in Poland a pharmacy will be open by a pharmacist, a citizen of another country, and will not be able to open a Polish entrepreneur pharmacy. In the light of so many doubts, the committee is not in favour of any of the parties to this market and asks for the rejection of the parliamentary bill, while recommending the inclusion of its assumptions in the work currently underway in the Ministry of Health on a major revision of the pharmaceutical law. I would just like to add that the committee recognises the urgent need for work on the revision of pharmaceutical law, in particular in the areas of: 1-percent market concentration, aggressive market operators' policy on the creation of new pharmacies, reverse distribution chain, namely export of medicines from Poland, supervision of clinics, the role of wholesalers in the trading system, the role of pharmacists in the market system. At the same time, the committee considers as an appropriate concept of ensuring the economic security of individual pharmacies in conflict with the current market model in Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On one side we have large, very rich pharmacy networks, on the other side we have individual, small drugstores that are fighting for survival today. Today you can call people of false awareness of all those who come out here and say that the project is bad and at the same time accuse lobbying those who want to help these small, small, individual, fragmented pharmacists. Ladies and gentlemen, let us not let ourselves believe that we in Poland can do less. Italy could, Austria could, France could, a whole crowd of countries could. We cannot regulate the pharmaceutical market, we cannot defend it against foreign capital and from entities, huge entities that are about to be taken over, even if they are Polish today, they will be taken over by foreign capital in a moment. And we can learn about this not from the Polish press, because in the Polish press people who submitted this project are being slandered, but in the Israeli press one can read that foreign networks have plans to invade the Polish market and have plans to take over 300 and 400 pharmacies a year, have plans to take over pharmacies capital in Poland. These are the true intentions of those who defend them today. Mr Meysztowicz stands in a row with those who at the embassy call for this bill not to be supported. In Germany, only a pharmacist can run a pharmacy? Maybe. Was there the same beating on the alarm? Is that where it is thought that these rules should be abandoned? Ladies and gentlemen, let's not get crazy. We gave up the grocery market, we gave up the drugstore market, and today it turns out that in the great transport entity of the German Polish product it is not possible to sell that Polish cream is repacked in a shop abroad, in Germany, to bring it here and call it German cream. And that's what we're gonna do with the pharmaceutical retail sale. Soon the Polish replacement will not be able to be bought because it will be a foreign network that will dictate what will be on the shelves. Ladies and gentlemen, let us regulate this market so that there is a balance between the large ones already functioning today and the small ones who are immersed in 1,5 m underwater and ask them to survive and function. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u352	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Pharmaceutical Law Act. The procedural history of the project discussed today is quite turbulent, and certainly in many it arouses great emotions, which has been expressed many times during previous work in committees. However, in my speech I will focus more on the meritory of this amendment, because at this stage I consider it more important for the process and for the citizens to refer to concrete solutions proposed. Among them are the possibility of having pharmacies only to pharmacists entitled to pursue their profession, the limitation of the number of pharmacies owned or co-owned to four and the geographical and demographic regulation of the distribution of newly created pharmacies. The fact that the proposed regulations apply only to newly issued authorisations and not to authorisations already issued and in force needs to be highlighted. The opponents of this project are of the opinion that today's revision, the solutions contained in it restrict freedom of business and violate the principle of competitiveness. They point to current regulations in this area, such as the requirement to hire a pharmacist as a pharmacy manager and the need to increase enforcement of the provision on maximum 1-percent concentration of pharmacies in the voivodship. However, as the applicants point out - and as we can all conclude from the observation of the current state of our pharmacy market - these are not completely solving problems in this area. Contrary to the regulations banning concentration on this market, there are places where the main object of this novel is on every corner, while we can hardly see it on the outskirts of cities. Such uneven deployment limits access to pharmacies and thus access to medicines, to those citizens for whom mobility is difficult and who live precisely in those underdeveloped areas. In my opinion, it is also necessary to openly acknowledge what has also been raised many times during the work so far. The pharmacological market is not a completely free market. Due to its specific characteristics, tasks and role, it is necessary to introduce rules to maximise the protection of public health. However, I do not agree with applicants who claim that one such mechanism is a change allowing only pharmacists who have the right to pursue their profession to be allowed to open a pharmacy, although I find it absurd to inherit the pharmacy. In my opinion, the pharmaceutical owner's philanthropic education, despite the assurances of the designers, does not guarantee that the motivations he will pursue in business will be in line with the principles of professional ethics and that it will not be a priority for him to make a profit. In view of the support expressed by the Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers for this novel and the assurance during the work of the committee that it is part of the concept of changes in the health department, near Free and Solidarni proposes that it be directed to further work.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u354	Dear Marshal! High Seym! I have the pleasure on behalf of the Committee on Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs to present information on the draft resolution on amending the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Sejm, contained in print no. 1333. I will say right away that this printing contains changes in two areas of the Sejm's Rules of Procedure, not the most important areas, and these are changes, as has been shown by the uncontroversial work on them so far. These changes stem from two laws: the Act on the making available of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments and the Act on the amendment of the Law on freedom of business, and implement the provisions of these laws in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. The first group of amendments shall specify the mode of making environmental information available and its protection, which, in accordance with the Act on the Sejm and the Senate, must be specified in the Rules of Procedure of those Chambers of Parliament. These amendments state that such information will be provided at the written request of the person concerned - of course, such a request may be made through the Sejm Information System. They shall also determine when, in certain cases, the Head of the Sejm Office may refuse to provide such information, as well as the procedure for appealing or requesting that the person concerned reconsiders such a case. As regards the second area of change, this is a requirement to assess the expected impact of a draft law or regulation on the activities of micro-entrepreneurs and small and medium-sized enterprises. According to the draft resolution, such an assessment will have to be a separate part of the justification for the draft law or regulation in the case of those documents which concern the property rights and obligations of traders or the rights and obligations of traders vis-à-vis public administrations. According to the provision of the resolution, this assessment will have to be a separate part of the justification, an assessment of the project's expected impact on the activities of micro-entrepreneurs, and the Marshal of the Sejm will be able to return the draft law to the applicant if the justification does not meet this requirement. The resolution introduces transitional provisions which say that the existing rules will apply to projects that have already been submitted, on which Parliament has already started work. The proposal for this draft resolution is submitted by the Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary Affairs and Immunity Committee, which has taken this resolution initiative as a result of suggestions made by Mr Marshal. At the committee meeting yesterday, this draft was unanimously supported and on behalf of the committee, I ask the High House to adopt it. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u425	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice on the submitted government bill amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. As a result of the changes made over the last several years in the rules on the functioning of the national capital market, the differences in the functioning of the stock market and the over-the-counter market have actually been overcome. The purpose of the bill is to abolish the unjustified division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market at present. Regulation will result in the inclusion of these types of markets in the same regulatory market concept. The consequences of the proposed changes are also the need to equip the supervisory authority with effective supervisory tools to ensure effective supervision of the safety and regularity of secondary trading of financial instruments, in particular in order to determine the scope of investments carried out by non-business entities in the territory of the Republic of Poland, which are parties to stock exchange transactions and which are not required under the regulations in force in Poland to record transactions concluded on derivatives on any accounts. The project also assumes that the interpretation doubts identified in the administrative procedures carried out so far for the authorisation of the stock exchange will be eliminated. As a consequence of the proposed amendments, the authorisation authority will become the Financial Supervision Authority. This authority will at the same time be competent to withdraw such authorisation and to prohibit the exercise of a regulated market. The draft assumes that the existing provisions will apply to the pending and unfinished proceedings concerning the application for authorisation. The Senate dealt with the Act of 10 February 2017 and introduced three amendments and the Finance Commission adopted it on 8 March. The Law and Justice Club fully supports the law on amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws together with the Senate amendments. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u427	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a position on the report of the Senate's committee on the Act amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, prints No 991, 1192, 1330 and 1346. The amendment provides for three fundamental changes. The first concerns the abolition of the division of the regulated market into the stock market and over-the-counter market. Another is the introduction of regulations on the institution and the derivatives account. It involves obtaining new powers by the Financial Supervision Authority. The third change envisaged by the project's authors is the change of the authority issuing the authorisation to operate the regulated market. This amendment takes away the law of the Finance Minister and, at the same time, imposes an obligation on the Financial Supervision Authority. The Senate Resolution of 3 March 2017 introduces three amendments to the draft. The first one adds a point that the committee shall forward annually to the European Securities and Markets Authority information on cases of application in the previous calendar year of sanctions, with the exception of withdrawal of the authorisation to conduct brokerage activities. The second amendment also adds a point concerning who prevents or impedes the conduct of the acts referred to in the Act from being punishable by imprisonment, restriction of liberty or fine. The last amendment concerns the addition of an article to the draft law, according to which the concept of a company operating a regulated market is added to the Act of 16 November 2000 on anti-money laundering and terrorist financing. The Committee on Public Finance addressed the amendments introduced by the Senate yesterday and proposes that they be adopted. The Modern Club agrees with the amendments made by the Senate and with the position of the Committee on Public Finance. The amendments are more corrective and their implementation is necessary for the proper functioning of the law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u557	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! I will only say, for example, about a few: the employee may file a lawsuit or an oral appeal to the minutes; the proceedings are pending before a district court; the hearing takes place within 2 weeks of the application; there are no limitations on evidence; the court has the right to take account of other claims available to the employee, even those which are not the result of the lawsuit. Even if the application is made whether or not the employee withdraws his claims, the court has the right in his interest - although he has made such a request - if he considers that the application infringes the interests of the worker, the application for withdrawal of the claim shall not be accepted. Therefore, we will not make this easier for employees if we transfer them to the group redress procedure, because employee proceedings are much more convenient for employees.
