ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u664	Statement on the 195th anniversary of the death of Józef Wybicki. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This year, exactly 10 March, we will celebrate the 195th anniversary of the death of Józef Wybicki - a Polish writer and politician, the author of the words of the Polish national anthem, Masurka Dąbrowskie. Józef Wybicki was born on 29 September 1747 in Bedomina near Kościerzyna in Kaszubach in a noble family. At the age of 18, he graduated from the Jesuit College in Old Scots near Gdansk and began his judicial practice. He participated in the election of King Stanisław August Poniatowski. In 1767 he was elected as a member of the Sejm and then began to make acquaintances with future members of the bar confederacy. At the Seym in 1768 he protested against the plans of Russian Member Mikołaj Repnin to bring about equality between dissidents and dissidents. He was also in favour of approving cardinal rights. However, his veto was not approved, the Sejm was suspended, and Wybicki had to leave Warsaw for threatening a Russian Member. Soon he joined the bar confederation to defend his country against Russia, as he claimed. As part of the Confederacy he was sent on many foreign missions, among others. Austria or the Netherlands. During Kościuszko's insurrection Wybicki was a major general of the Pomeranian militia. He participated in the defence of Warsaw against the Prussian-Russian assault. After his third dissection, he went to Paris for exile. Then he joined Napoleon's army and joined the political life of the Duchy of Warsaw. Four years later, he received the title of a French baron. Wybicki was also a poet. In his literary works he addressed issues related to politics and social life. He did not think very highly of his poetic talent, but he wrote a poem that knows every Pole - Mazurek Dąbrowskie. The present anthem of Poland was founded in Reggio, Italy in July 1797 especially for the farewell of General Jan Henryk Dąbrowski going to meet Napoleon. He was sung to the tune of an old mazurka. In time, the words of the poem have undergone a number of modifications to its content to the changing historical circumstances. Originally it was a military song, then a national song, performed during patriotic celebrations. Since the Battle of Grochow in 1831, during the November Uprising, it began to be regarded as a national anthem. During the period of Poland's loss of independence, Dąbrowski's Mazurek belonged to the most popular patriotic songs. In 1926 he was declared an official national anthem. Wybicki died on 10 March 1822 in Mannies near Śrem, he was buried in Brodnica near Śrem, where his empty grave is now located. In 1923, the remains were moved to Poznań, to the crypt at St. Wojciech, where the well-deserved Great Poles lie. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u665	They, fighting, thought of us. We, living, remember them. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 1 March 2017, we celebrated once again the national holiday established on the initiative of the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński, restoring to the Pantheon the Heroes of the Narodowy soldiers cursed - Poles who challenged communist dictatorship in defence of the highest values. By Act of 3 February 2011 (announced on 15 February 2011), the Sejm of the Republic of Poland established on 1 March the National Day of Remembrance of the Cursed Soldiers. This festival is an expression of tribute to the soldiers of the second conspiracy for their testimony of bravery, unbreakable patriotic attitude and attachment to patriotic traditions, for the blood shed in defence of their homeland. The National Day of Remembrance of Cursed Soldiers is also an expression of homage to numerous local communities whose patriotism and constant willingness of victims for the independence idea allowed the continuation of resistance for many years. It is an expression of gratitude for the fight against the sovietization of the country of soldiers who have never received any reward - for decades, branded by the name of the bandits and surrounded by contempt, they have lived only in the memory of their closest and faithful comrades in arms. The National Day of Remembrance of the Cursed Soldiers is a modest reparation and payment of moral debt to the most steadfast. Now they're back on history cards, although their names and aliases were to be erased forever. They were given a shameful death, and they were deprived of graves, so that they would not become a model for the generations to follow. As a member of the Parliamentary Team of Tradition and Remembrance of Cursed Soldiers, I would like to thank teachers and educators of children and young people, heads of schools and cultural institutions, representatives of various backgrounds for the organisation of the National Day of Remembrance, for their special attention in the first days of March to commemorate the heroes of the anti-communist underground. I would like to express my special thanks to the director Remigiusz Koźminski and the teachers of the Municipal School Complex in Radziejow for the wonderful and moving verbal and musical assembly - a beautiful spectacle presented at the Diocesan Museum in Włocławek. I thank the young people who showed patriotism - a beautiful attitude of love for their homeland, setting an example for their peers. I would also like to express my thanks to the representatives of the National School of Education and Higher Education in Włocławek for this valuable initiative to commemorate the heroes of the anti-communist underground from the region.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u666	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! It is worth mentioning today an important anniversary. On 9 March 1983, she was interned after a forced strike in Gdańsk Shipyard, accused of continuing union activities under martial law, and was brought before a court in Grudziądz Anna Valentynowicz. She was sentenced to a year and a half incarcerated. In 1983, on the radio station of Polish Radio Free Europe, journalist Wacław Pomorski said: "Military authorities are bringing Anna Valentynowicz to justice, accusing her of the greatest crime in the eyes of Communists - continuing union activity after 13 December 1981. In other words, she was accused of being active in defending workers' rights at a time when she needed extraordinary courage and fearless attitude to say out loud what she thought. Anna Valentynowicz was characterized by a steadfast attitude. After she got out of prison, she said: "I'm not doing anything wrong, I'm just helping others survive. And I'm gonna do it, you gotta do it. We must help one another without harming anyone, on the contrary, we must correct evil. At the moment, I believe that I should help people who are imprisoned, and that is my task - said Anna Valentynowicz immediately after she was released from prison in 1984. In communist times, when the majority of those involved in the opposition went through moments of test of character, when some were allowed to break or voluntarily cooperate with the SB, reporting to their colleagues for money, they were asleep. Anna Valentinovich witnessed true heroism. Not all of them, including those who consider themselves leaders of Solidarity, were able to afford it. In July 2006, the Gdańsk Institute of National Remembrance revealed that Anna Valentynowicz had been monitoring over 100 officers and secret security officers, and in 1981 she was planned to be killed by drug poisoning. Let's remember today about this unbreakable hero. Let's remember Anna Solidarity. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u667	Declaration on the correct marking and technical state of the roads within railway crossings. Dear Marshal, According to the data of the Railway Transport Office, 223 accidents occurred in 2016. As a result, 53 people were killed and 34 were badly injured. The situation is also similar in such places as the so-called. wild crossings, i.e. those in which railway tracks are not permitted. In these cases 142 people died and 50 were badly injured. It happens that accidents that occur in these places are attributed to the railways, while the irregularities that led them are not always on their side. Attention should be paid to irregularities such as incorrect location of vehicles, difficulties in obtaining information from road managers by the railway infrastructure manager, e.g. the results of the measurement of traffic intensity, and the lack of cooperation and mutual information of railway infrastructure managers, police and road managers on the checks carried out. The Railway Transport Office proposes that the road infrastructure manager should also inform the relevant railway infrastructure manager or the President of the UTK after indicating the irregularity at the crossing. I think it is also worth considering this occasion in the case of the protection of the youngest, the idea of the President of the UTK, who proposed to the Minister of National Education to include in every stage the education of children and young people information provided to students in the framework of the office's ABC Rail Campaign. On the other hand, I call on the High House to draw attention to this and to analyse the phenomenon and the problem in depth. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u668	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Next Saturday, March 11th, we celebrate the Day of the Sołtys, an annual Polish holiday which is an expression of gratitude and respect for the chiefs for their work, and at the same time a good opportunity to draw the attention of society to the role of the soultys in the Polish village. The institution of the Council has been known in Poland since the Middle Ages. The centuries-old tradition and the ever-important position of the head of the rural community indicate that this function is extremely needed. Importantly, the council elects the villagers, which is the result of which most often becomes a person who shows outstanding knowledge of local affairs. Today, the duties of the chiefs, apart from representing the council outside, include: convening rural meetings, implementing the resolutions of the municipality council, as well as collecting agricultural and forestry taxes. On the eve of this year's celebrations, I would like to express my sincere thanks and wishes to the villagers of all the regions of our country, and in particular to the Opolskie Voivodeship Councils, which I am a representative of. As a former self-government officer, I am convinced that every councilman is a unique person. You have been given special trust by the people of your towns. The fulfilment of social functions is a difficult task, which in this case requires additional and specific personality characteristics, because this country, among all local authorities, is closest to your local communities. Every day you deal directly with all the problems of the people of your community, often these are not easy meetings. Acting as a tax collector is a difficult and responsible task, which can often cause negative associations. It is also important that the Council must have mediation capacity in order to respond and take the right stance in conflict cases. However, thanks to your resourcefulness and your diligence, you are able to cope with all of this. At this point I would like to remind you of the important achievement of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party, i.e. the adoption of a new regulation on the functioning of the Social Fund. Separation from the municipal budget of the measures guaranteed for the implementation of projects to improve the lives of villagers with an increase of 15% the number of municipalities in which the fund operates has strengthened the position of the local authorities. The amendment was made by virtue of the Law of 21 February 2014 on the Social Fund. I would like to point out that, according to this law, the Sołecki Fund was allocated PLN 129 million from the state budget in 2016, and in 2017 this amount is already at PLN 132 million. At this point it is impossible to ignore the fact that on 27 January this year. At first reading, the Parliament's draft law on the Councils and the Soviet Councils was rejected by the votes of the Members of the Law and Justice. The project envisaged strengthening the system of settlements as supporting bodies in rural communities. According to the project, the powers of the local authorities were to be re-established. At the same time, the project sought to strengthen the position of the Chief of Staff, which thanks to the new competences was to obtain the powers of a public officer, and assumed to grant, regardless of the diet and reimbursement of the travel expenses, remuneration relating to the functions performed. Unfortunately, the project was rejected. To conclude, I would like to sincerely thank all the Councils for their efforts, work and dedication. I wish you perseverance, success, satisfaction in fulfilling your daily duties and every success. Let the feeling of a well-fulfilled duty to the villagers be the source of your satisfaction and joy.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-09-sejm-37-2.u669	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Bielsko-Bialski Aero Club is a highly rated branch of Polish Aero Club. It has titled members, professional instructors, modern equipment and rich infrastructure. He runs an aircraft, glider and parachute section. It has e.g. Airport in Aleksandrowice. In the near vicinity, in Międzybrodzie Żywiecki, there is a Górska Szybowcowa School, which is one of the main centres of glider training in Poland. The school has a perfectly located airport on Mount Żar in Beskid Mały. All of this constitutes an excellent training base for possible use by the emerging troops of the Territorial Defence Forces. One such branch is to be established in Bielsko-Biała by 2020. Among the tasks facing soldiers of territorial defence are among others. carrying out military activities in cooperation with operational forces, civil and property protection, search actions or cooperation with elements of the state's defence system. From this point of view, I would like to point out that the Ministry of National Defence should take into account the involvement of the potential of the Bielsko-Bialski Aeroclub and the Mountain Stool School for pro-defence training. The directions for possible cooperation should be discussed in detail with the Aeroclub and school authorities by a well-targeted team of the Military Territorial Defence and Air Force Command. It will also be useful to invite the Civil Aviation Office and other entities from so-called. a third sector whose participation in the project would be justified. The agreement drawn up should take into account both the defence needs of the Republic of Poland and the interests of the Aero Club and the school, which will certainly be willing to share their experience, infrastructure and equipment under appropriate conditions.
